| 208 | Ask the client first | 1. Total turnover (on receipts, without fixed-asset sales) for 2025 and 2026 to date, and the year the business started. This decides § 19 and § 20. | — | intake questions, actual amount/time tests quoted in corresponding rule rows | — |
| 209 | Ask the client first | 2. Accruals or cash accounting, and the tax office's letter allowing cash accounting. | — | intake questions, actual amount/time tests quoted in corresponding rule rows | — |
| 210 | Ask the client first | 3. Monthly or quarterly filer, whether the one-month extension is in place, and the special prepayment paid in February. | — | intake questions, actual amount/time tests quoted in corresponding rule rows | — |
| 211 | Ask the client first | 4. Any exempt supplies (rent, finance, medical, education) alongside taxable ones, and how input tax was split during the year. | — | intake questions, actual amount/time tests quoted in corresponding rule rows | — |
| 212 | Ask the client first | 5. Assets bought in the last five years (ten for buildings) whose use changed, that were sold, or that came from a small-business year. | — | intake questions, actual amount/time tests quoted in corresponding rule rows | — |
| 213 | Ask the client first | 6. Sales to consumers in other EU states, and whether the business is registered for the one-stop shop or the EU small business scheme. | — | intake questions, actual amount/time tests quoted in corresponding rule rows | — |

## Complete old-topic destinations

Every actual old nonblank unit is retained below. Exact wording is distinguished from corrected law, historical examples and specific unverified assertions; no separately verified platform body is invented.

| Actual comparator / LF | Exact old unit | Verified destination or precise historical limit |
|---|---|---|
| 7f5ea7a4c28d:15 | > **General reference only.** This skill is general tax reference material for AI-assisted work. It has not been reviewed for any specific business's facts. Do not rely on it to file, pay or take a position without review by a qualified professional in Germany. | Exact wording retained at current LF18, Germany: the annual VAT return and the special schemes, tax year 2026 |
| 7f5ea7a4c28d:17 | **What this skill does.** [germany-vat-return](1cd70888078b153ec7f3-germany-vat-return.md) classifies transactions and fills in the monthly or quarterly advance return (Umsatzsteuer-Voranmeldung, form USt 1 A) under standard taxation. This skill picks up the cases that skill refuses and the year-end: the annual VAT return for 2026, the small business scheme, cash accounting, the one-month extension, partial exemption, input tax correction, reverse charge, the one-stop shop, penalties and the e-invoicing dates. | Exact wording retained at current LF20, Germany: the annual VAT return and the special schemes, tax year 2026 |
| 7f5ea7a4c28d:19 | **Sibling skills.** [germany-vat-return](1cd70888078b153ec7f3-germany-vat-return.md) for the box-by-box advance return and the supplier library. [germany-einvoice](01e17161e950cdac0cb5-germany-einvoice.md) for XRechnung, ZUGFeRD and archiving. [de-income-tax](577d735e0609a07b30dc-de-income-tax.md) and [de-einkommensteuer-freelancer](66f1b38ff566ac023dd1-de-einkommensteuer-freelancer.md) for income tax, where a small business reports income gross and a VAT-registered business reports it net. [de-corporate-tax](8a9e86e5dcaca8e0e7b2-de-corporate-tax.md) for a GmbH's profit. | DE-L15 source-corrected cash-EÜR sibling treatment retained at current LF22; old net-only shorthand not retained as a cash method. |
| 7f5ea7a4c28d:23 | **Every business (Unternehmer) taxed in Germany files an annual VAT return** for the calendar year, electronically, in which it computes the tax or the surplus itself (§ 18(3) UStG). The tax period is the calendar year (§ 16(1) UStG), or the part of it in which the business traded (§ 16(3)). The return counts as a tax assessment subject to review (§ 168 AO), so no notice follows unless the tax office departs from it. If the return lowers the tax below the prepayments or shows a refund, that effect starts only when the tax office agrees, which needs no form; the refund is then paid without a separate claim (§ 168 sentences 2 and 3 AO; USt 2 E 2026, lines 124 to 126). | Exact wording retained at current LF26, Who this applies to |
| 7f5ea7a4c28d:25 | **Who does not file it.** | Exact wording retained at current LF28, Who this applies to |
| 7f5ea7a4c28d:26 | - **A small business using § 19 UStG.** § 19(1) sentence 2 switches off the filing duties of § 18(1) to (4) UStG, which includes the annual return. This applies from tax year 2024 (§ 27(39) UStG). Two exceptions keep a return due: the tax office asks for one (§ 149(1) sentence 2 AO), or the business owes tax as a buyer, for example under reverse charge or on an intra-Community acquisition (§ 18(4a) UStG). The 2026 form instructions say the same. | Exact wording retained at current LF29, Who this applies to |
| 7f5ea7a4c28d:27 | - **A foreign business whose only German supplies go through the one-stop shop.** For those supplies § 18(1) to (4) does not apply (§ 18j(4) sentence 4 UStG). | Exact wording retained at current LF30, Who this applies to |
| 7f5ea7a4c28d:29 | **The small business scheme (§ 19 UStG, rules in force since 1 January 2025).** A business based in Germany makes tax-free supplies if its total turnover was not above EUR 25,000 in the previous calendar year and is not above EUR 100,000 in the current year. Tax-free means no VAT on invoices and no input tax deduction. Total turnover (Gesamtumsatz, § 19(2)) is counted on receipts and leaves out sales of fixed assets and some exempt supplies. Three rules decide most cases (UStAE 19.1): | Exact wording retained at current LF32, Who this applies to |
| 7f5ea7a4c28d:30 | - Previous year above EUR 25,000: standard taxation from 1 January of the current year, even if the current year stays low. | Exact wording retained at current LF33, Who this applies to |
| 7f5ea7a4c28d:31 | - Previous year within EUR 25,000: the scheme runs until current-year turnover passes EUR 100,000. The supply that crosses the line is already taxed in full, and from then on normal invoicing, input tax and advance returns apply. | Exact wording retained at current LF34, Who this applies to |
| 7f5ea7a4c28d:32 | - First year of business: the limit is EUR 25,000 in the current year, not EUR 100,000. The supply that crosses EUR 25,000 is already taxed. | Exact wording retained at current LF35, Who this applies to |
| 7f5ea7a4c28d:34 | A small business can give up the scheme by a statement to the tax office up to the last day of February of the second year after the year concerned. The waiver binds for at least five calendar years and can then be revoked from the start of a later calendar year (§ 19(3)). | Exact wording retained at current LF37, Who this applies to |
| 7f5ea7a4c28d:36 | **The EU small business scheme.** A German business that wants the small business exemption in another member state joins a special reporting procedure at the Bundeszentralamt für Steuern (BZSt), which issues a small-business identification number (§ 19a(1) UStG). Its EU-wide annual turnover must not exceed EUR 100,000 in the previous or the current year. It files a quarterly turnover report with the BZSt within one month after each quarter (§ 19a(3)). It must tell the BZSt within 15 working days if EU turnover passes EUR 100,000; participation ends at that point (§ 19a(4)). A business based elsewhere in the EU can use the German exemption on the same EUR 100,000 test with a small-business number from its home state (§ 19(4)). | Exact wording retained at current LF39, Who this applies to |
| 7f5ea7a4c28d:38 | **Cash accounting (Istversteuerung, § 20 UStG).** The normal rule taxes supplies when they are made (Sollversteuerung, § 16(1)). On application the tax office can let a business tax receipts instead if any one of these holds: total turnover of the previous year was not more than EUR 800,000; the business is released from bookkeeping under § 148 AO; or, for those supplies only, it practises a liberal profession under § 18(1) no. 1 EStG. Input tax is not affected: it is deductible once the supply is made and the invoice is held, not when it is paid (§ 15(1) no. 1 UStG). Only VAT on a payment made before the supply waits for both the invoice and the payment. | Exact wording retained at current LF41, Who this applies to |
| 7f5ea7a4c28d:40 | **Out of scope here.** VAT groups (Organschaft), the margin schemes (§§ 25, 25a), the flat rate for farmers (§ 24), and the one-stop shops for non-EU businesses (§ 18i) and imports (§ 18k). Refer these. | Exact wording retained at current LF43, Who this applies to |

| 7f5ea7a4c28d:77 | **The annual return is a full recomputation, not a sum of the quarters.** Under § 18(3) UStG the business recomputes the year's tax from § 16(1) to (4) and § 17 UStG: output tax on the year's supplies, plus tax owed as a buyer, minus deductible input tax, plus or minus input tax corrections. The advance returns are only prepayments on it. So the annual return is where errors in the advance returns are put right, where § 15a corrections of up to EUR 6,000 are made (§ 44(3) UStDV), and where a partial-exemption key estimated during the year is replaced with the final one. | DE-L14 correction at current LF65/80/94: annual6,000 simplification retained with the actual44(3) sentence2 sale/3(1b) disposal-period exception. |
| 7f5ea7a4c28d:79 | **How the 2026 form (USt 2 A) ends.** Section K collects the totals. Line 124 (Kz 816) is the remaining tax or surplus for the year. Line 125 is the prepayment total for 2026, the "Vorauszahlungssoll", including any special prepayment. Line 126 (Kz 820) is the balance: line 124 minus line 125. A positive balance is the closing payment (Abschlusszahlung); a negative one is a refund. Line 125 is what was declared or assessed for the advance returns, not what was actually paid; late or missing payments are chased separately. | Exact wording retained at current LF82, The taxable base |
| 7f5ea7a4c28d:81 | **The special prepayment in the annual figures.** A monthly filer with the one-month extension pays 1/11 of the previous year's advance payments by 10 February (§§ 47, 48(2) UStDV). It is credited in the last advance return of the year (§ 48(4) UStDV) and so it sits inside the line 125 total. A quarterly filer pays none. | Exact wording retained at current LF84, The taxable base |
| 7f5ea7a4c28d:83 | **Small business: what counts.** Total turnover is the sum of taxable supplies counted on receipts, less exempt financial and insurance supplies and some others listed in § 19(2), and without sales of fixed assets. Supplies on which the customer owes the tax under § 13b count in; services the small business buys under § 13b do not (UStAE 19.2(2)). A small business still owes VAT on imports, intra-Community acquisitions and reverse-charge purchases (UStAE 19.1(1)). | Exact wording retained at current LF86, The taxable base |
| 7f5ea7a4c28d:85 | **Moving into standard taxation.** When a small business moves into standard taxation, a supply made in the new year is taxed in full even if the customer paid in advance in the old year. That after-tax goes in line 39 (Kz 317) of the annual return and in line 46 (Kz 65) of the advance return for the period of the supply (USt 1 E 2026, line 46). A supply made in the small-business year stays tax-free even when it is paid later. Input tax on assets bought while exempt can come back over the § 15a period: the 2026 form lists a change of scheme under the small business rules as a change in circumstances for § 15a (USt 2 A, line 100). | Exact wording retained at current LF88, The taxable base |
| 7f5ea7a4c28d:87 | **Cash accounting.** The tax arises when the fee is received. When the method changes, no supply may be taxed twice or escape tax (§ 20 sentence 3). A supply made in a cash-accounting year stays on cash accounting if it is paid after a later switch to accruals (USt 2 A 2026 instructions, line 21). | Exact wording retained at current LF90, The taxable base |
| 7f5ea7a4c28d:89 | **Partial exemption (§ 15(4) UStG).** When a purchase is used for both taxable supplies and exempt supplies that bar deduction, the share linked to the exempt supplies is not deductible. Allocate directly where you can. Use a reasonable estimate otherwise. Split by turnover only if no more precise economic allocation is possible. For buildings, split by floor area unless another method is more precise. The share is fixed for the year in the annual return. | Exact wording retained at current LF92, The taxable base |
| 7f5ea7a4c28d:91 | **Input tax correction (§ 15a UStG).** If the use of an asset changes within five years of first use (ten years for land and buildings), the input tax deducted on it is corrected by one fifth (or one tenth) a year for each year of change (§ 15a(1) and (5)). A sale or a private withdrawal during the period is also a change (§ 15a(8)). The UStDV simplifications are in the table: nothing below EUR 1,000 of input tax, nothing for a change under 10 points unless above EUR 1,000, and only in the annual return up to EUR 6,000. | DE-L14 correction at current LF65/80/94: annual6,000 simplification retained with the actual44(3) sentence2 sale/3(1b) disposal-period exception. |
| 7f5ea7a4c28d:93 | **Domestic reverse charge (§ 13b UStG).** The customer owes the tax for, among others, services from EU suppliers under § 3a(2), works and services from foreign suppliers, construction services to a business that itself provides construction services, building cleaning on the same basis, and mobile phones, tablets and chips from EUR 5,000. The customer declares output tax and, if entitled, deducts the same amount as input tax. A small business that receives such services owes the tax and cannot deduct it. The boxes and the supplier patterns are in [germany-vat-return](1cd70888078b153ec7f3-germany-vat-return.md). | Exact wording retained at current LF96, The taxable base |

| 7f5ea7a4c28d:109 | \| One-stop shop return and payment \| End of the month after each quarter: 30 April, 31 July, 31 October, 31 January. Payment must have reached the Bundeskasse by then. A nil return is due for a quarter with no sales. For Q4 2026 the date is Sunday 31 January 2027: file and pay by Friday 29 January 2027, because no official text moves this date \| § 18j(4) UStG; BZSt OSS page \| | Exact wording retained at current LF112, Deadlines and filing |
| 7f5ea7a4c28d:111 | **How to file.** The annual return goes to the local tax office electronically, authenticated, through ELSTER (§ 18(3) UStG; § 87a(6) AO). Paper only with permission, to avoid hardship. Foreign businesses add Anlage UN. The one-stop shop return goes to the BZSt through the BZSt online portal. | Exact wording retained at current LF114, Deadlines and filing |
| 7f5ea7a4c28d:113 | **One-stop shop (§ 18j UStG).** A business established only in Germany that sells goods at a distance or supplies telecom, broadcasting or electronic services to consumers in other member states charges the customer's country's VAT once the EU-wide total passes EUR 10,000 (§ 3c(4)); the very sale that crosses it is the first one. Instead of registering in each country it can declare that VAT quarterly through the one-stop shop at the BZSt. The notice to take part must reach the BZSt before the quarter from which the scheme is used (§ 18j(1) sentence 3), so registration normally starts on the first day of the quarter after the application. Taking part covers all member states and all such supplies. Corrections to an OSS return within three years go in a later OSS return. Repeated late filing leads to exclusion (§ 18j(6)). | Exact wording retained at current LF116, Deadlines and filing |
| 7f5ea7a4c28d:117 | - **Late annual return.** The tax office may set a late filing surcharge (§ 152(1) AO). It must set one if the return is not filed within 14 months after the year ends, for 2026 by 29 February 2028, unless the tax office extended the deadline or the tax is nil, negative or not above the prepayments (§ 152(2) no. 1 and (3)). The amount is 0.25% per started month of the tax less the prepayments, at least EUR 25 per started month, capped at EUR 25,000 (§ 152(5) sentence 2 and (10)). For the monthly or quarterly advance returns the amount is discretionary instead (§ 152(8)). | Exact wording retained at current LF120, Penalties and interest |
| 7f5ea7a4c28d:118 | - **Late payment.** 1% per started month on the overdue tax rounded down to a multiple of EUR 50 (§ 240(1) AO). This runs on a late closing payment and on late advance payments. | Exact wording retained at current LF121, Penalties and interest |
| 7f5ea7a4c28d:119 | - **Interest.** Where an assessment differs from the prepayments, the difference carries 0.15% for each full month from 1 April 2028 for tax year 2026 (§ 233a(2) AO, 15 months after the year end; § 238(1a) AO). | Exact wording retained at current LF122, Penalties and interest |
| 7f5ea7a4c28d:120 | - **Fines.** Not paying an advance payment, a closing balance or an OSS payment in full and on time is an administrative offence with a fine up to EUR 30,000 (§ 26a(1) and (3) UStG). Deliberately or recklessly not issuing a required invoice, or not keeping invoices for eight years: up to EUR 5,000. Deliberately or recklessly not keeping, for two years, the invoices and payment records that § 14b(1) sentence 5 requires: up to EUR 1,000 (§ 26a(2) nos. 1 to 3 and (3)). | Exact wording retained at current LF123, Penalties and interest |
| 7f5ea7a4c28d:121 | - **Correcting an error.** An error in an advance return can be corrected in the annual return, as in Example 1. Deliberate under-declaration is tax evasion and outside this skill. | Exact wording retained at current LF124, Penalties and interest |
| 7f5ea7a4c28d:125 | - **Foreign businesses with German VAT duties** file the annual return like a German business, with Anlage UN and, from 2026, a foreign address for notices (BMF letter of 29 December 2025 on the 2026 form, para. 4). The tax office for each country is in the pack README. | Exact wording retained at current LF128, Cross-border points |
| 7f5ea7a4c28d:126 | - **EU businesses using the German small business exemption** need the small-business number from their home state and EU turnover within EUR 100,000 (§ 19(4)). They waive it in their home state, not in Germany (§ 19(5)). | Exact wording retained at current LF129, Cross-border points |
| 7f5ea7a4c28d:127 | - **German businesses using another state's small business exemption** go through the BZSt procedure in § 19a, with the quarterly report and the 15-working-day notice above. | Exact wording retained at current LF130, Cross-border points |
| 7f5ea7a4c28d:128 | - **One-stop shop.** Sales declared through the OSS are taxed in the customer's state and are not part of the German output tax. | Exact wording retained at current LF131, Cross-border points |
| 7f5ea7a4c28d:129 | - **Reverse charge on services from abroad** applies to a small business too, which then must file an advance return for that period and an annual return for the year (§ 18(4a)). | Exact wording retained at current LF132, Cross-border points |
| 7f5ea7a4c28d:135 | Muster GmbH is a quarterly filer on accruals, with no extension. Its four 2026 advance returns declared output tax of EUR 38,000 and input tax of EUR 29,750, so prepayments of EUR 8,250 (Q1 2,550, Q2 1,900, Q3 2,200, Q4 1,600). Preparing the annual return, the accountant finds a December 2026 sale of EUR 10,000 net, invoiced but booked in January 2027. It belongs to 2026. Output tax for the year is 38,000 + 1,900 = EUR 39,900. Line 124 (Kz 816) is 39,900 - 29,750 = EUR 10,150. Line 125 is EUR 8,250. Line 126 (Kz 820) is a closing payment of EUR 1,900. | Exact wording retained at current LF138, Example 1: A GmbH's 2026 annual return with one correction |
| 7f5ea7a4c28d:137 | The GmbH files itself on Friday 14 May 2027, so the balance is due one month later, Monday 14 June 2027. If it paid two months late, the late payment surcharge would be 1% x 1,900 x 2 = EUR 38. If it had instead filed on 15 October 2027, three months started after the 2 August deadline. Should the tax office set a surcharge, 0.25% of 1,900 is EUR 4.75 a month, below the EUR 25 minimum, so 3 x 25 = EUR 75. | Exact wording retained at current LF140, Example 1: A GmbH's 2026 annual return with one correction |

| 7f5ea7a4c28d:170 | Lena is a self-employed translator, a liberal profession under § 18(1) no. 1 EStG. Her 2024 total turnover was within EUR 25,000, so in 2025 she used § 19. Her total turnover for 2025 was EUR 31,200, counted on receipts and including the advance below (UStAE 19.2(1)). That is above EUR 25,000 but below EUR 100,000, so 2025 stayed tax-free and she filed no VAT return for it. Because 2025 passed EUR 25,000, every supply she makes from 1 January 2026 is taxed at 19%. Her 2025 turnover is far below EUR 800,000, and she is a liberal professional, so the tax office can let her tax receipts (§ 20 nos. 1 and 3); she applies for it. | Exact wording retained at current LF173, Example 2: A translator leaves the small business scheme and uses cash accounting |
| 7f5ea7a4c28d:172 | Her 2025 tax was nil, so she files quarterly (§ 18(2)). In Q1 2026 she receives EUR 8,925 for 2026 work (EUR 7,500 net plus EUR 1,425 VAT) and EUR 1,800 for a December 2025 job. The December job was supplied while she was exempt, so it carries no VAT. A client paid her EUR 2,000 in November 2025 as an advance for a job she delivered in February 2026. The supply is in 2026, so the advance is taxed in the period of the supply (USt 1 E 2026, line 46; USt 2 E 2026, line 39): 2,000 x 19 / 119 = EUR 319.33, in Kz 65 of the advance return and line 39 (Kz 317) of the annual return. Her input tax on 2026 invoices for the quarter is EUR 380. Q1 payment: 1,425 + 319.33 - 380 = EUR 1,364.33, due Friday 10 April 2026. She must already be able to receive e-invoices. For 2026 supplies she may still send paper invoices, or PDF invoices with the client's consent, to German business clients until 31 December 2026 (§ 27(38) no. 1); for 2027 supplies the relief continues to 31 December 2027 as long as her 2026 total turnover stays within EUR 800,000 (no. 2). | Exact wording retained at current LF175, Example 2: A translator leaves the small business scheme and uses cash accounting |

| 62b7639a5a68:18 | > **General reference only.** This skill is general tax reference material for AI-assisted work. It has not been reviewed for any specific business's facts. Do not rely on it to file, pay or take a position without review by a qualified professional in Germany. | Exact wording retained at current LF18, Germany: the annual VAT return and the special schemes, tax year 2026 |
| 62b7639a5a68:20 | **What this skill does.** [germany-vat-return](1cd70888078b153ec7f3-germany-vat-return.md) classifies transactions and fills in the monthly or quarterly advance return (Umsatzsteuer-Voranmeldung, form USt 1 A) under standard taxation. This skill picks up the cases that skill refuses and the year-end: the annual VAT return for 2026, the small business scheme, cash accounting, the one-month extension, partial exemption, input tax correction, reverse charge, the one-stop shop, penalties and the e-invoicing dates. | Exact wording retained at current LF20, Germany: the annual VAT return and the special schemes, tax year 2026 |
| 62b7639a5a68:22 | **Sibling skills.** [germany-vat-return](1cd70888078b153ec7f3-germany-vat-return.md) for the box-by-box advance return and the supplier library. [germany-einvoice](01e17161e950cdac0cb5-germany-einvoice.md) for XRechnung, ZUGFeRD and archiving. [de-income-tax](577d735e0609a07b30dc-de-income-tax.md) and [de-einkommensteuer-freelancer](66f1b38ff566ac023dd1-de-einkommensteuer-freelancer.md) for income tax, where a small business reports income gross and a VAT-registered business reports it net. [de-corporate-tax](8a9e86e5dcaca8e0e7b2-de-corporate-tax.md) for a GmbH's profit. | DE-L15 source-corrected cash-EÜR sibling treatment retained at current LF22; old net-only shorthand not retained as a cash method. |
| 62b7639a5a68:26 | **Every business (Unternehmer) taxed in Germany files an annual VAT return** for the calendar year, electronically, in which it computes the tax or the surplus itself (§ 18(3) UStG). The tax period is the calendar year (§ 16(1) UStG), or the part of it in which the business traded (§ 16(3)). The return counts as a tax assessment subject to review (§ 168 AO), so no notice follows unless the tax office departs from it. If the return lowers the tax below the prepayments or shows a refund, that effect starts only when the tax office agrees, which needs no form; the refund is then paid without a separate claim (§ 168 sentences 2 and 3 AO; USt 2 E 2026, lines 124 to 126). | Exact wording retained at current LF26, Who this applies to |
| 62b7639a5a68:28 | **Who does not file it.** | Exact wording retained at current LF28, Who this applies to |
| 62b7639a5a68:29 | - **A small business using § 19 UStG.** § 19(1) sentence 2 switches off the filing duties of § 18(1) to (4) UStG, which includes the annual return. This applies from tax year 2024 (§ 27(39) UStG). Two exceptions keep a return due: the tax office asks for one (§ 149(1) sentence 2 AO), or the business owes tax as a buyer, for example under reverse charge or on an intra-Community acquisition (§ 18(4a) UStG). The 2026 form instructions say the same. | Exact wording retained at current LF29, Who this applies to |
| 62b7639a5a68:30 | - **A foreign business whose only German supplies go through the one-stop shop.** For those supplies § 18(1) to (4) does not apply (§ 18j(4) sentence 4 UStG). | Exact wording retained at current LF30, Who this applies to |
| 62b7639a5a68:32 | **The small business scheme (§ 19 UStG, rules in force since 1 January 2025).** A business based in Germany makes tax-free supplies if its total turnover was not above EUR 25,000 in the previous calendar year and is not above EUR 100,000 in the current year. Tax-free means no VAT on invoices and no input tax deduction. Total turnover (Gesamtumsatz, § 19(2)) is counted on receipts and leaves out sales of fixed assets and some exempt supplies. Three rules decide most cases (UStAE 19.1): | Exact wording retained at current LF32, Who this applies to |
| 62b7639a5a68:33 | - Previous year above EUR 25,000: standard taxation from 1 January of the current year, even if the current year stays low. | Exact wording retained at current LF33, Who this applies to |
| 62b7639a5a68:34 | - Previous year within EUR 25,000: the scheme runs until current-year turnover passes EUR 100,000. The supply that crosses the line is already taxed in full, and from then on normal invoicing, input tax and advance returns apply. | Exact wording retained at current LF34, Who this applies to |
| 62b7639a5a68:35 | - First year of business: the limit is EUR 25,000 in the current year, not EUR 100,000. The supply that crosses EUR 25,000 is already taxed. | Exact wording retained at current LF35, Who this applies to |
| 62b7639a5a68:37 | A small business can give up the scheme by a statement to the tax office up to the last day of February of the second year after the year concerned. The waiver binds for at least five calendar years and can then be revoked from the start of a later calendar year (§ 19(3)). | Exact wording retained at current LF37, Who this applies to |
| 62b7639a5a68:39 | **The EU small business scheme.** A German business that wants the small business exemption in another member state joins a special reporting procedure at the Bundeszentralamt für Steuern (BZSt), which issues a small-business identification number (§ 19a(1) UStG). Its EU-wide annual turnover must not exceed EUR 100,000 in the previous or the current year. It files a quarterly turnover report with the BZSt within one month after each quarter (§ 19a(3)). It must tell the BZSt within 15 working days if EU turnover passes EUR 100,000; participation ends at that point (§ 19a(4)). A business based elsewhere in the EU can use the German exemption on the same EUR 100,000 test with a small-business number from its home state (§ 19(4)). | Exact wording retained at current LF39, Who this applies to |
| 62b7639a5a68:41 | **Cash accounting (Istversteuerung, § 20 UStG).** The normal rule taxes supplies when they are made (Sollversteuerung, § 16(1)). On application the tax office can let a business tax receipts instead if any one of these holds: total turnover of the previous year was not more than EUR 800,000; the business is released from bookkeeping under § 148 AO; or, for those supplies only, it practises a liberal profession under § 18(1) no. 1 EStG. Input tax is not affected: it is deductible once the supply is made and the invoice is held, not when it is paid (§ 15(1) no. 1 UStG). Only VAT on a payment made before the supply waits for both the invoice and the payment. | Exact wording retained at current LF41, Who this applies to |
| 62b7639a5a68:43 | **Out of scope here.** VAT groups (Organschaft), the margin schemes (§§ 25, 25a), the flat rate for farmers (§ 24), and the one-stop shops for non-EU businesses (§ 18i) and imports (§ 18k). Refer these. | Exact wording retained at current LF43, Who this applies to |

| 62b7639a5a68:80 | **The annual return is a full recomputation, not a sum of the quarters.** Under § 18(3) UStG the business recomputes the year's tax from § 16(1) to (4) and § 17 UStG: output tax on the year's supplies, plus tax owed as a buyer, minus deductible input tax, plus or minus input tax corrections. The advance returns are only prepayments on it. So the annual return is where errors in the advance returns are put right, where § 15a corrections of up to EUR 6,000 are made (§ 44(3) UStDV), and where a partial-exemption key estimated during the year is replaced with the final one. | DE-L14 correction at current LF65/80/94: annual6,000 simplification retained with the actual44(3) sentence2 sale/3(1b) disposal-period exception. |
| 62b7639a5a68:82 | **How the 2026 form (USt 2 A) ends.** Section K collects the totals. Line 124 (Kz 816) is the remaining tax or surplus for the year. Line 125 is the prepayment total for 2026, the "Vorauszahlungssoll", including any special prepayment. Line 126 (Kz 820) is the balance: line 124 minus line 125. A positive balance is the closing payment (Abschlusszahlung); a negative one is a refund. Line 125 is what was declared or assessed for the advance returns, not what was actually paid; late or missing payments are chased separately. | Exact wording retained at current LF82, The taxable base |
| 62b7639a5a68:84 | **The special prepayment in the annual figures.** A monthly filer with the one-month extension pays 1/11 of the previous year's advance payments by 10 February (§§ 47, 48(2) UStDV). It is credited in the last advance return of the year (§ 48(4) UStDV) and so it sits inside the line 125 total. A quarterly filer pays none. | Exact wording retained at current LF84, The taxable base |
| 62b7639a5a68:86 | **Small business: what counts.** Total turnover is the sum of taxable supplies counted on receipts, less exempt financial and insurance supplies and some others listed in § 19(2), and without sales of fixed assets. Supplies on which the customer owes the tax under § 13b count in; services the small business buys under § 13b do not (UStAE 19.2(2)). A small business still owes VAT on imports, intra-Community acquisitions and reverse-charge purchases (UStAE 19.1(1)). | Exact wording retained at current LF86, The taxable base |
| 62b7639a5a68:88 | **Moving into standard taxation.** When a small business moves into standard taxation, a supply made in the new year is taxed in full even if the customer paid in advance in the old year. That after-tax goes in line 39 (Kz 317) of the annual return and in line 46 (Kz 65) of the advance return for the period of the supply (USt 1 E 2026, line 46). A supply made in the small-business year stays tax-free even when it is paid later. Input tax on assets bought while exempt can come back over the § 15a period: the 2026 form lists a change of scheme under the small business rules as a change in circumstances for § 15a (USt 2 A, line 100). | Exact wording retained at current LF88, The taxable base |
| 62b7639a5a68:90 | **Cash accounting.** The tax arises when the fee is received. When the method changes, no supply may be taxed twice or escape tax (§ 20 sentence 3). A supply made in a cash-accounting year stays on cash accounting if it is paid after a later switch to accruals (USt 2 A 2026 instructions, line 21). | Exact wording retained at current LF90, The taxable base |
| 62b7639a5a68:92 | **Partial exemption (§ 15(4) UStG).** When a purchase is used for both taxable supplies and exempt supplies that bar deduction, the share linked to the exempt supplies is not deductible. Allocate directly where you can. Use a reasonable estimate otherwise. Split by turnover only if no more precise economic allocation is possible. For buildings, split by floor area unless another method is more precise. The share is fixed for the year in the annual return. | Exact wording retained at current LF92, The taxable base |
| 62b7639a5a68:94 | **Input tax correction (§ 15a UStG).** If the use of an asset changes within five years of first use (ten years for land and buildings), the input tax deducted on it is corrected by one fifth (or one tenth) a year for each year of change (§ 15a(1) and (5)). A sale or a private withdrawal during the period is also a change (§ 15a(8)). The UStDV simplifications are in the table: nothing below EUR 1,000 of input tax, nothing for a change under 10 points unless above EUR 1,000, and only in the annual return up to EUR 6,000. | DE-L14 correction at current LF65/80/94: annual6,000 simplification retained with the actual44(3) sentence2 sale/3(1b) disposal-period exception. |
| 62b7639a5a68:96 | **Domestic reverse charge (§ 13b UStG).** The customer owes the tax for, among others, services from EU suppliers under § 3a(2), works and services from foreign suppliers, construction services to a business that itself provides construction services, building cleaning on the same basis, and mobile phones, tablets and chips from EUR 5,000. The customer declares output tax and, if entitled, deducts the same amount as input tax. A small business that receives such services owes the tax and cannot deduct it. The boxes and the supplier patterns are in [germany-vat-return](1cd70888078b153ec7f3-germany-vat-return.md). | Exact wording retained at current LF96, The taxable base |

| 62b7639a5a68:112 | \| One-stop shop return and payment \| End of the month after each quarter: 30 April, 31 July, 31 October, 31 January. Payment must have reached the Bundeskasse by then. A nil return is due for a quarter with no sales. For Q4 2026 the date is Sunday 31 January 2027: file and pay by Friday 29 January 2027, because no official text moves this date \| § 18j(4) UStG; BZSt OSS page \| | Exact wording retained at current LF112, Deadlines and filing |
| 62b7639a5a68:114 | **How to file.** The annual return goes to the local tax office electronically, authenticated, through ELSTER (§ 18(3) UStG; § 87a(6) AO). Paper only with permission, to avoid hardship. Foreign businesses add Anlage UN. The one-stop shop return goes to the BZSt through the BZSt online portal. | Exact wording retained at current LF114, Deadlines and filing |
| 62b7639a5a68:116 | **One-stop shop (§ 18j UStG).** A business established only in Germany that sells goods at a distance or supplies telecom, broadcasting or electronic services to consumers in other member states charges the customer's country's VAT once the EU-wide total passes EUR 10,000 (§ 3c(4)); the very sale that crosses it is the first one. Instead of registering in each country it can declare that VAT quarterly through the one-stop shop at the BZSt. The notice to take part must reach the BZSt before the quarter from which the scheme is used (§ 18j(1) sentence 3), so registration normally starts on the first day of the quarter after the application. Taking part covers all member states and all such supplies. Corrections to an OSS return within three years go in a later OSS return. Repeated late filing leads to exclusion (§ 18j(6)). | Exact wording retained at current LF116, Deadlines and filing |
| 62b7639a5a68:120 | - **Late annual return.** The tax office may set a late filing surcharge (§ 152(1) AO). It must set one if the return is not filed within 14 months after the year ends, for 2026 by 29 February 2028, unless the tax office extended the deadline or the tax is nil, negative or not above the prepayments (§ 152(2) no. 1 and (3)). The amount is 0.25% per started month of the tax less the prepayments, at least EUR 25 per started month, capped at EUR 25,000 (§ 152(5) sentence 2 and (10)). For the monthly or quarterly advance returns the amount is discretionary instead (§ 152(8)). | Exact wording retained at current LF120, Penalties and interest |
| 62b7639a5a68:121 | - **Late payment.** 1% per started month on the overdue tax rounded down to a multiple of EUR 50 (§ 240(1) AO). This runs on a late closing payment and on late advance payments. | Exact wording retained at current LF121, Penalties and interest |
| 62b7639a5a68:122 | - **Interest.** Where an assessment differs from the prepayments, the difference carries 0.15% for each full month from 1 April 2028 for tax year 2026 (§ 233a(2) AO, 15 months after the year end; § 238(1a) AO). | Exact wording retained at current LF122, Penalties and interest |
| 62b7639a5a68:123 | - **Fines.** Not paying an advance payment, a closing balance or an OSS payment in full and on time is an administrative offence with a fine up to EUR 30,000 (§ 26a(1) and (3) UStG). Deliberately or recklessly not issuing a required invoice, or not keeping invoices for eight years: up to EUR 5,000. Deliberately or recklessly not keeping, for two years, the invoices and payment records that § 14b(1) sentence 5 requires: up to EUR 1,000 (§ 26a(2) nos. 1 to 3 and (3)). | Exact wording retained at current LF123, Penalties and interest |
| 62b7639a5a68:124 | - **Correcting an error.** An error in an advance return can be corrected in the annual return, as in Example 1. Deliberate under-declaration is tax evasion and outside this skill. | Exact wording retained at current LF124, Penalties and interest |
| 62b7639a5a68:128 | - **Foreign businesses with German VAT duties** file the annual return like a German business, with Anlage UN and, from 2026, a foreign address for notices (BMF letter of 29 December 2025 on the 2026 form, para. 4). The tax office for each country is in the pack README. | Exact wording retained at current LF128, Cross-border points |
| 62b7639a5a68:129 | - **EU businesses using the German small business exemption** need the small-business number from their home state and EU turnover within EUR 100,000 (§ 19(4)). They waive it in their home state, not in Germany (§ 19(5)). | Exact wording retained at current LF129, Cross-border points |
| 62b7639a5a68:130 | - **German businesses using another state's small business exemption** go through the BZSt procedure in § 19a, with the quarterly report and the 15-working-day notice above. | Exact wording retained at current LF130, Cross-border points |
| 62b7639a5a68:131 | - **One-stop shop.** Sales declared through the OSS are taxed in the customer's state and are not part of the German output tax. | Exact wording retained at current LF131, Cross-border points |
| 62b7639a5a68:132 | - **Reverse charge on services from abroad** applies to a small business too, which then must file an advance return for that period and an annual return for the year (§ 18(4a)). | Exact wording retained at current LF132, Cross-border points |
| 62b7639a5a68:138 | Muster GmbH is a quarterly filer on accruals, with no extension. Its four 2026 advance returns declared output tax of EUR 38,000 and input tax of EUR 29,750, so prepayments of EUR 8,250 (Q1 2,550, Q2 1,900, Q3 2,200, Q4 1,600). Preparing the annual return, the accountant finds a December 2026 sale of EUR 10,000 net, invoiced but booked in January 2027. It belongs to 2026. Output tax for the year is 38,000 + 1,900 = EUR 39,900. Line 124 (Kz 816) is 39,900 - 29,750 = EUR 10,150. Line 125 is EUR 8,250. Line 126 (Kz 820) is a closing payment of EUR 1,900. | Exact wording retained at current LF138, Example 1: A GmbH's 2026 annual return with one correction |
| 62b7639a5a68:140 | The GmbH files itself on Friday 14 May 2027, so the balance is due one month later, Monday 14 June 2027. If it paid two months late, the late payment surcharge would be 1% x 1,900 x 2 = EUR 38. If it had instead filed on 15 October 2027, three months started after the 2 August deadline. Should the tax office set a surcharge, 0.25% of 1,900 is EUR 4.75 a month, below the EUR 25 minimum, so 3 x 25 = EUR 75. | Exact wording retained at current LF140, Example 1: A GmbH's 2026 annual return with one correction |

| 62b7639a5a68:173 | Lena is a self-employed translator, a liberal profession under § 18(1) no. 1 EStG. Her 2024 total turnover was within EUR 25,000, so in 2025 she used § 19. Her total turnover for 2025 was EUR 31,200, counted on receipts and including the advance below (UStAE 19.2(1)). That is above EUR 25,000 but below EUR 100,000, so 2025 stayed tax-free and she filed no VAT return for it. Because 2025 passed EUR 25,000, every supply she makes from 1 January 2026 is taxed at 19%. Her 2025 turnover is far below EUR 800,000, and she is a liberal professional, so the tax office can let her tax receipts (§ 20 nos. 1 and 3); she applies for it. | Exact wording retained at current LF173, Example 2: A translator leaves the small business scheme and uses cash accounting |
| 62b7639a5a68:175 | Her 2025 tax was nil, so she files quarterly (§ 18(2)). In Q1 2026 she receives EUR 8,925 for 2026 work (EUR 7,500 net plus EUR 1,425 VAT) and EUR 1,800 for a December 2025 job. The December job was supplied while she was exempt, so it carries no VAT. A client paid her EUR 2,000 in November 2025 as an advance for a job she delivered in February 2026. The supply is in 2026, so the advance is taxed in the period of the supply (USt 1 E 2026, line 46; USt 2 E 2026, line 39): 2,000 x 19 / 119 = EUR 319.33, in Kz 65 of the advance return and line 39 (Kz 317) of the annual return. Her input tax on 2026 invoices for the quarter is EUR 380. Q1 payment: 1,425 + 319.33 - 380 = EUR 1,364.33, due Friday 10 April 2026. She must already be able to receive e-invoices. For 2026 supplies she may still send paper invoices, or PDF invoices with the client's consent, to German business clients until 31 December 2026 (§ 27(38) no. 1); for 2027 supplies the relief continues to 31 December 2027 as long as her 2026 total turnover stays within EUR 800,000 (no. 2). | Exact wording retained at current LF175, Example 2: A translator leaves the small business scheme and uses cash accounting |

## Writer checks and limits

The full current Guide and actual first-introduction/main comparators are read. Every figure/rule is bound to selected actual clauses, exact2026 annual/advance form passages and the stated2025 UStAE edition. The large46,936LF UStAE was acquired whole but only the recorded selected current paragraphs were read; historical paragraphs inside its long footnotes are not relabelled current. Raw PDFs are immutable; exact quotes retain nativeLF extraction including two-column form layout. The two original oa examples recompute from fictional inputs, with explicit monetary rounding; no return or payment performed. DE-L14/15 root corrections are bound to exact51433fb5 successor; prior draft/support retained under history. Friday29January is a conservative Guide operating date, not an invented statutory deadline. No independent or country PASS.
